Running scheduled sampling on the GNN (rows_gnnmixed alpha 0.5 from payload_gnn_m15, 6 workers) -> fine-tune -> loop -> alpha 0.25.
- T-115b THE INTERIOR MODEL IS AT THE TRUTH'S OWN NOISE FLOOR; THE FINAL LAYER IS 40x ABOVE IT. Valid-40 residual of the definitive learned closure vs the organizers' 1e9-sample floor (<= sigma^2/1e9 per unit): L8 1.5e-10 vs floor 3.4e-10; L12 2.5e-10 vs 2.2e-10; L14 3.2e-10 vs 1.8e-10; L15 6.4e-9 vs 1.6e-10. So the interior one-step errors are predicted to the limit of the labels (the 99% is really ~100%); the final-layer deficit is structural and specific to the final read. Ridge probe (final_layer_cliff_probe.py): quadratic ridge on the 51 features reaches 35% at L15 / 50% at L12; adding the previous layer's one-step error pushed through W (W d, W^2 d^2, |W||d|) adds NOTHING (32.6%) -> the cliff is NOT transported error. Suspect: the final layer's k3/k4 come from a different, cheaper read (_cp_k3 with cp3_context[3] + _push_cubic_diagonal; no full pre_k21 read) whose error depends on off-diagonal third-order structure the features do not carry. Next: force truth k3 / k4 / v at layer 15 only inside the oracle recursion and see which state removes the 2.23e-8 one-step error.
- T-115c FINAL-LAYER DECOMPOSITION (final_layer_force_diag.sh; r35, oracle recursion = exact carried means + consistent covariance, truth state forced at layer 15 ONLY, scored vs the independent 4M truth, noise-corrected): none 2.32e-8 | true v 1.10e-8 (-53%) | true k3 1.87e-8 (-20%) | true k4 1.62e-8 (-30%) | true v,k3,k4 together -4.9e-10 (= the scoring floor: ZERO). So the final layer's entire one-step error is its per-unit STATE error (variance, skew, kurtosis at the final pre-activation); the univariate ReLU map itself is exact. The card's pre-variance error under the oracle recursion grows smoothly with depth (0.045% L4, 0.20% L8, 0.56% L12, 0.78% L14, 0.85% L15) - no anomaly at 15 - so the learned closure's cliff (98.5% at L14 -> 71.4% at L15) is an INPUT deficit: the final read builds no K21 plane and no K22/K31 planes, so 12 of the 31 mech columns are zero at L15 (age0-3, older, energy, k21_row/col, cp4_k31/k22_row, k22_row, k31_row) - exactly the plane-energy quantities that at interior layers let the model infer the dropped/under-scaled plane structure behind the state errors. T-115d test: fill those 12 columns at L15 with the W^2-weighted fan-in average of layer 14's columns (GNN_FINAL_PUSH=1, train_gnn_oracle.py; deployable at ~1e7 flops) -> GNN_M15_FP. (d) pushed plane columns (layer 14's 12 plane-derived mech columns W^2-fan-in-averaged through W15 into the final layer's zero columns; GNN_FINAL_PUSH=1): L15 70.8% at epoch 25 = no gain (instrument hook RETRO_CL_FINAL_PUSH=1 and builder BUILD_FINAL_PUSH=1 exist, unused). (e) residual anatomy (final_residual_anatomy.py, 8 valid nets): the L15 residual is spread (top 10% of units = 75% of its energy, vs 61% at L14), corr(|res|,|target|) +0.62 (heteroscedastic, not outliers), no mech column correlates beyond -0.16 (v, pre_k4), flat across gate buckets. (f) per-net gain (final_residual_gain.py, 12 valid nets): slope of target on prediction 1.004 +- 0.041 per net; an oracle per-net gain lifts 71.5% -> 71.7% (nothing); the interior slopes 0.98-0.99. So the residual is per-unit, orthogonal to the prediction, and not a net-level scale. State-error anatomy (nets 35/100, oracle recursion vs 32M truth): dv/v per unit is mean ~0 with sd 0.2% (L8) -> 0.7-0.8% (L14/15), uncorrelated with v (a per-unit, non-uniform error at EVERY late layer, not just the final); dk3 is shrunk (corr -0.6 with the true k3, sd ~10% of rms); dk4 is -40..-52% with corr -0.97 (the kurtosis-cap defect, a pure scale). - T-115j VARIANCE ERROR IS OFF-DIAGONAL-BORN (r35, oracle recursion, 32M truth): the pre-variance error at L12/L14/L15 (0.56/0.78/0.85% rel rms) is explained only 3-4% by the previous layer's per-unit POST-VARIANCE errors pushed through W^2; 96-97% comes from the OFF-DIAGONAL post-covariance error at l-1 pushed bilinearly (diag(W^T dC W)). With T-100 (the covariance residual is low-rank and mean-aligned, dC ~ a m^T + m a^T) this gives dv_l,i ~ 2 pre_m_l,i (W^T a)_i: a per-unit vector a at l-1 pushed through W times the pre-mean - representable by the message channel + encoder products, which the interior evidently learns. - T-116g ROOT CAUSE OF THE WHOLE CLOSED-LOOP FAILURE = TARGET LEAKAGE IN THE TRAINING TAPE. In the oracle-recursion rows (rows_meanleak*, all refits since T-106 and the learned closure), the tape row of layer l was built AFTER the oracle mean correction, so raw20 column 8 (reported mean/sigma) and column 9 (standardized reported-minus-Gaussian mean) contained the CORRECTED mean: stored col9 minus the loop's col9 equals target/sigma with corr +1.000 at L1/L4/L8 (+0.998 at L14). Every interior "removal" measured on those rows (tape-operator refits ~60%, learned closure 99%) was the model reading the answer; the FINAL layer has no hook, so its tape is honest and its 71% is the true capability. Proof chain: in the loop at L1 the step's input equals the stored row except cols 8/9 (max rel diff 6e-2), the standalone fnp step on the loop's own input reproduces the loop's delta exactly (corr 1.000) but has corr 0.16 with torch's prediction on the stored row; the F1 loop's depth profile equals the uncorrected base host at every layer. So the loop never had a control problem - it had a wrong model. Consequences: T-106..T-116f interior numbers are void; the scheduled-sampling rows (loop-generated, tape pre-correction) were honest (hence their 59-66%). FIX: estimator_retro.py builds the tape from _pre_mean_row under RETRO_MEANLEAK_MEAN=1; regenerating honest oracle rows (rows_meanleak_mech15b), retraining, then the loop.

- T-116m BUILT (not yet trained): four pre-covariance contraction inputs per unit per layer - (C m)_i/(sigma_i|m|), sum_j C_ij^2/v_i^2, (C m^2)_i/(sigma_i|m^2|), (C g)_i/(sigma_i|g|) - computed in fnp (_covfeat_fnp; ~4 matvecs), dumped as covfeat_l with RETRO_DUMP_FEAT, saved by both generators as covfeat.npy, consumed by the trainer with GNN_COVFEAT=1, fed in-loop with RETRO_CL_COVFEAT=1. Rationale: T-115j (the late-layer variance error is 97% off-diagonal-born) and T-116i (the 51 inputs are an information limit at ~65%). Needs honest rows regenerated with the dump (queued after the alpha-0 rows to stay within the memory allowance).
- T-116l HONEST ABLATION (20 epochs, 64ch): TAPE-ONLY (all 31 mech columns zeroed at every layer) -> final 64.5%, L8 59% / L12 64% / L14 65%, vs the full 51-input model 66-67%. The 31 mechanistic columns add only 1-2 points honestly; the 20-number tape already carries the information the model uses. The missing ~35% per layer (mostly the off-diagonal-born variance error, T-115j) is not in either set -> new observables are needed (covariance contractions queued, T-116m).
- T-116l NO-PLANE-COLUMNS honest ablation: 65.4% (full 66%): the 12 plane-derived columns add ~0.5 points. Information ranking with the current observables: tape 64.5% -> +mech 66-67%; the remaining ~33% of the fresh error per layer needs observables not yet computed.
- T-116n WHAT THE HONEST MODEL MISSES = THE VARIANCE ERROR'S MEAN EFFECT (net 35, F1 model, 32M truths, linearised Gaussian-ReLU derivatives): the model's per-unit residual correlates +0.65..+0.67 with dmean/dv * dv at L8/L12/L14/L15, only -0.23..-0.33 with the k3 effect and +0.1..+0.2 with the k4 effect; a joint linear fit explains 46-52% of the residual energy, dominated by dv. So the model captures the systematic k3/k4 errors and misses the off-diagonal-born per-unit variance error (T-115j). PLAN: variance LABELS for all 200 nets by MC at ~1M samples on MPS (per-unit rel noise 0.14% vs the 0.5-0.85% error; ~10 s/net) -> an auxiliary/variance head (dv per unit) -> carry a VARIANCE correction in the loop (the phase-2 memory law: half the v error removed = -25%/-35% with the operator kept).
- T-116o VARIANCE HEAD DESIGN: (a) labels: mc_truth_mps.py at 1M samples for ranks 35-234 -> vlabels/ (run_vlabels.sh, ~1 h MPS shared); (b) trainer: a NEXT-LAYER variance head evaluated at the post side of layer l-1 from the GNN state e_{l-1} pushed through W_l (message channels), predicting dv_l / v_l per unit (label = truth v_l - card v_l from the oracle rows' sigma^2), trained jointly with the mean head; (c) instrument: stash dv_next at layer l-1's hook, add it to pre_variance and the diagonal of pre_covariance at layer l's pre side (the _retro_force_state location) before the ReLU map; (d) candidate builder later. Expected: the mean head's residual is mostly the v effect (T-116n), and the phase-2 law says half the v error removed = -25%/-35%.
- T-116h SCHEDULED SAMPLING ROUND 1 (honest): GNN_H_SS05AGG (aggregate oracle + alpha-0.5 rows, fine-tuned from HF1, 12 ep; valid on the aggregate 64.7%) loop, 1e9 frame: r35 2.382e-8 (-3.6% vs HF1 2.471), r100 2.666e-8 (+1.6% vs 2.623) -> neutral to slightly positive. 8M frame: 2.478e-8 / 2.783e-8. after_honest2 (alpha 0 DAgger from SS05AGG + exposure input) running.
- T-116o BUILT: variance head end to end - trainer (GNN_VLABELS=<dir>, GNN_VLOSS; next-layer head from e_l through W_{l+1}; reports held-out removal of the relative v error), exporter (msgv/vh/vrms keys when trained), instrument (_gnn_step computes dv_next when the payload has the head and the hook set _GNN["W_next"]; RETRO_CL_VHEAD=1 applies v <- v(1+mix dv) and C_ii at the next layer's pre side, interior and final; RETRO_CL_VHEAD_MIX default 0.5; dump cl_dv_l). chain_vhead.sh waits for the labels (run_vlabels.log VLABELS_DONE) -> GNN_HV_F1 -> loop at mix 0/0.5/1 -> 1e9-frame ranking (chain_vhead.log VHEAD_DONE). Candidate builder not yet updated for the v-head (needs W_next + the pre-side hooks).
- T-116o BUILDER UPDATED: build_gnn_candidate.py now sets _GNN["W_next"] before the interior step, applies the variance head's correction before both ReLU-map coefficient calls (interior and final; BUILD_VHEAD_MIX default 0.5; _VHEAD_MIX literal), and resets the head's state per prediction; inert when the payload has no head (cand_gnn_hf1_v2.py builds). Position matches the instrument (before the coefficient call, after the reads).
- T-116o SMOKE-VALIDATED: a 2-epoch v-head model trains, exports with the head keys, and the loop applies cl_dv at layers 2-14 (+ final) without error (guard added: no head output at the final layer's step). Real training waits for the 200 labels (chain_vhead.sh).
- T-116j DAGGER (alpha 0 rows from SS05AGG's own loop, aggregate oracle + 0.5 + 0 rows = 600 nets, 12 ep from SS05AGG): held-out on the aggregate 49.6% of the total (transported + fresh) error at every layer; loop r35 2.464e-8 (8M frame; SS05AGG 2.478, HF1 2.537): -0.5%. The on-policy rounds are giving ~1% each - the transported residual is NOT being learned away by aggregation alone. The variance head (T-116o) is the mechanistic lever; training now on the 200 labels.
- T-116j DAGGER 1e9 frame: r35 2.408e-8 / r100 2.652e-8 (SS05AGG 2.382 / 2.666; HF1 2.471 / 2.623): the on-policy program is FLAT within +-3%. Reading: the transported residual is the model's own unobservable residual pushed forward; features that cannot predict the fresh error cannot predict its transport either - aggregation cannot fix an information limit. Only new observables can (variance head T-116o, covariance contractions T-116m).
- T-116o VARIANCE HEAD v1 RESULT: held-out removal of the relative v error 47% (L12) / 53% (L14) / 55% (L15) (label-noise ceiling ~97%); mean head unchanged 67.1%. LOOP (8M frame): mix 0 r35 2.490e-8 / r100 2.740e-8 (the HV mean head alone, -2% vs HF1); mix 0.5 r35 2.860e-8 / r100 4.167e-8 -> WORSE. Applied corrections have the right sign (corr +0.73..+0.79 with the oracle-row label, slope +1.1..1.2) = alignment ~0.75-0.8, inside the zone the phase-2 law flags ("the v channel punishes off-direction proxies: 0.5-0.77 alignment makes the card 2-3x worse; needs > ~0.9"). Also the head was trained on ORACLE-state labels while the loop's v error differs (partial mean correction upstream). NEXT (T-116p): (1) loop-consistent labels = train on the aggregate incl. the alpha-0 rows (their sigma is the loop's v, so (v_truth - v_loop)/v_loop is the label automatically); (2) a wider head (GNN_VCM=48, GNN_VHID=128); (3) then a PRE-SIDE head v2 fed with layer l+1's own pre-side features (tape minus cols 8/9, mech) + messages.
- T-116o VARIANCE HEAD v1, 1e9 frame: mix 0 r35 2.407e-8 / r100 2.584e-8 (BEST mean-only loop so far; deployed 2.520 / 2.589; HF1 2.471 / 2.623); mix 0.5 2.817 / 4.045; mix 1.0 5.061 / 7.612. Arithmetic says a right-signed correction with corr 0.79 and 70% amplitude should CUT the v error to ~62% rms, so the loop's degradation is not the head's noise alone: the mean head, trained on uncorrected-v states, already compensates part of the v error's mean effect and the two double-count. T-116p co-adaptation: after_vhead2.sh regenerates ORACLE rows with the HV_F1 head active at mix 1.0 (rows_oracle_vh), retrains the MEAN head on them with the variance head FROZEN (GNN_VFREEZE=1, GNN_VLOSS=0), loops at mix 1.0 / 0.5, ranks, gates. In parallel: HVAGG (loop-consistent labels + 48/128 head) and the covfeat trainings.
- T-116p INCIDENT: the first rows_oracle_vh set was contaminated - the killed after_vhead.sh (best mix 0.0) had already launched drivers at mix 0, they kept running after the kill, and after_vhead2's mix-1.0 drivers skipped their finished rows -> archived as rows_oracle_vh_MIXED_INVALID; regenerated from scratch at mix 1.0 (after_vhead2 relaunched). Verified: under the generator's exact env the head fires (cl_dv at layers 2-14; a direct row run changes v by 0.5-1.0% rms at L12-15). Lesson: pkill the drivers AND the workers when killing an orchestrator.
- T-116m COVARIANCE CONTRACTIONS: NULL. With the 4 pre-covariance contraction inputs: teacher-forced 67.6% (control on the same rows 67.1%; +0.5), loop r35 2.549e-8 (8M frame; HF1 2.537e-8). The card's own covariance structure carries no information about the variance ERROR (which is the structure of the covariance error, T-115j).
- T-116b EXPOSURE INPUT: on the 600-net aggregate 52.2% (vs 49.6% without), but loop r35 2.822e-8 (8M; DAGGER 2.464e-8) -> WORSE: on the 160 oracle rows the exposure feature equals the full target while in the loop it is the model's own delta - a distribution mismatch the aggregate cannot hide. Drop the exposure input (or restrict its training to on-policy rows).
- T-116b EXPOSURE r100 3.060e-8 (DAGGER 2.742): dead. after_honest2 complete. T-116q BUILT + RUNNING: a BILINEAR variance channel - dv_{l+1} += sum_c w_c (W^T u_c(e_l)) (W^T w_c(e_l)) / v_{l+1} (the pushed-product form of a low-rank covariance residual, T-100/T-115j), 8 channels, alongside the wider MLP head; trainer GNN_VBILIN=k, exporter keys vu/vw/vbil, instrument stashes q at layer l and divides by v_{l+1} at the application site (both sites), builder updated. chain_vhead_bil.sh -> GNN_HVBIL -> loops at mix 0.5/1.0 -> ranking.
- T-116m COVFEAT loop 1e9 frame: r35 2.463e-8 / r100 2.610e-8 (HF1 2.471 / 2.623): -0.3% / -0.5%, null confirmed.
- T-116p HVAGG (loop-consistent labels from the alpha-0 rows + 48/128 head, 30 ep from HV_F1): v-error removal L8 27% / L12 56% / L14 63% / L15 65% (v1: 20/47/53/55); mean head on the aggregate 49.5%. LOOP (8M frame): mix 0.25 r35 2.538e-8 / r100 3.421e-8; mix 0.5 r35 3.213e-8 -> still harmful, r100 much worse. A 63%-accurate head at mix 0.25 should shrink the v error to ~85% rms, so the harm is not the residual v error itself: suspects = (a) the head is NOISE at early layers (L4 1%, L8 27%) and early-layer noise compounds through depth; (b) the mean head double-counts. T-116r: layer window RETRO_CL_VHEAD_LAYERS (instrument, both sites) - test the HVAGG head at layers 12-15 only (vhead_window_test.sh). Co-adaptation (HV2) and the bilinear head (HVBIL) pending.
- T-116s DECISIVE: the honest loop (HV mean head, variance head OFF) + the TRUE variance forced at every layer with HALF the error removed -> r35 1.738e-8 (8M frame) vs 2.490e-8 for the loop alone: -30%. The variance channel WORKS inside the closed loop with an accurate supply and WITHOUT retraining the mean head; the learned heads (alignment ~0.75-0.8) hurt purely because of their accuracy - the phase-2 alignment law (> ~0.9 needed) holds in the loop. So the whole problem reduces to ONE number: the variance head's alignment (63-65% removal now; need > 81%). Routes: bilinear channel (running), ensembling heads, richer pre-side inputs, 4M labels for the early layers, train/valid gap check.
- T-116s/t THE VARIANCE CHANNEL IN THE LOOP, MEASURED WITH THE TRUTH (r35, 1e9 frame; loop alone 2.407e-8): TRUE v, half the error removed -> 1.826e-8 (-24%); TRUE v, all of it -> 3.465e-8 (+44%, WORSE). Partial beats full even with the truth (T-80's law, now inside the closed loop): the mean head trained on uncorrected-v states compensates part of the v effect. So (a) the mix must stay ~0.5 until the mean head is co-adapted (HV2 running), and (b) the learned head's harm at mix 0.25-0.5 is its accuracy ON LOOP STATES: HVAGG head per-net removal on ORACLE rows r35 75/80/80% (L12/14/15, corr 0.87-0.89), r100 72/80/84% (corr 0.85-0.92), but on the LOOP's own rows (alpha 0) r35 59/61/62% (corr 0.78-0.80), r100 42/53/53% (corr 0.65-0.74); the loop's v error is ~30% larger than the oracle's (transported mean-residual effects, unobservable) -> the head is punished exactly on those states. Gap check (train vs valid on oracle rows): 53/59/59 vs 47/54/56 -> underfit, not overfit. The bar in the 1e9 frame ~1.55e-8 (-36%); the true-v-half gives -24%: the variance channel alone will not reach the bar; it needs the co-adapted mean head and/or the k3/k4 state channels (labels exist in vlabels).
- T-116u BUILT: next-layer SKEW/KURTOSIS heads (same structure as the variance head; labels = (k3_truth - k3_card)/rms_l, (k4_truth - k4_card)/rms_l from vlabels/, GNN_KLABELS=1, GNN_KLOSS; exporter keys msgk/kh/krms; instrument RETRO_CL_KHEAD=1 / RETRO_CL_KHEAD_MIX applies dk3 to diag(pre_k21) and dk4 to pre_k4 at the interior pre side and to final_k3/final_k4 at the final read, under the same layer window). Smoke-tested end to end.
- T-116u CEILING (r35, 8M frame, loop alone 2.490e-8): TRUE k3 at half -> 2.269e-8 (-9%); TRUE k4 at half -> 2.630e-8 (+6%, WORSE: the mean head already compensates the systematic k4 error, corr -0.97, so a state k4 correction double-counts). The variance channel (-30% at half) is the one that matters; k3 is a minor helper; k4 is not a channel. Smoke of the k heads: k4 head 78-81% after 2 epochs (systematic), k3 41-53%; the first loop smoke blew up (mse 2e-3) from a units bug (labels were normalised by the TRUTH's per-layer rms, unavailable at deploy) - fixed: labels and deploy now use the CARD's per-layer rms.
- T-116r WINDOW (HVAGG head at layers 12-15 only, 1e9): mix 0.5 r35 2.689e-8 / r100 3.681e-8; mix 1.0 4.357 / 6.019 -> the window does not rescue it. T-116u 1e9: true k3 half 2.174e-8 (-10%), true k4 half 2.893e-8 (+20%), true v+k3+k4 half 3.722e-8 (+55%): only v (and mildly k3) are channels; k4 must NOT be corrected in the state. Alignment ceiling on loop states for any oracle-part predictor = rms(oracle error)/rms(loop error) = 0.62/0.80 = 0.775 (r35 L15): the loop's extra v error is the mean residual's transported effect. Running T-116v: TRUE v half + iid noise 0.3% / 0.5% -> is per-unit noise what kills the learned head?
- T-116t OUTLIERS: none. The HVAGG head's predictions are well behaved (max ~3%, p99 ~1-2%, top-1% units carry 8% of the error energy on oracle rows, 14-16% on loop rows). Its per-unit error rms: oracle rows 0.20-0.26% (L12/L14), loop rows 0.32-0.45% (the transported mean residual, diffuse). So the harm is diffuse residual, not wild units; the truth+noise ladder (T-116v) measures the loop's sensitivity to exactly that.
- T-116v NOISE SENSITIVITY (r35, 1e9): TRUE v at half at every layer 1.826e-8; + iid relative noise 0.3% at every layer -> 2.33e-7 (10x worse); 0.5% -> 6.1e-7. The variance channel is hypersensitive to UNSTRUCTURED per-unit noise (the natural v error is structured and the mean head co-adapts to it; iid noise is not). Caveat: uniform 0.3% is 7x the natural error at L4, so early layers may dominate the damage - rerunning at layers 12-15 only (truev_noise12.sh: none / 0.1% / 0.3%). Implication: a learned head must have residual << 0.1-0.2% rms on loop states to be safe, i.e. > ~90% removal; current heads leave 0.32-0.45%.
- T-116x BUILT: a COVARIANCE-LEVEL head - per-unit factor a_l from e_l (GNN_ACOV=1); training loss on the INDUCED next-layer relative variance change 2 (W^T m_post)(W^T a)/v_{l+1} against the dv labels; deploy (RETRO_CL_ACOV=1, RETRO_CL_ACOV_MIX) adds mix (a m^T + m a^T) to the post covariance at the closed-loop hook, so the diagonal AND off-diagonals move coherently (the mean-aligned rank-2 structure of the true covariance residual, T-100/T-115j) instead of an iid diagonal poke that the recursion amplifies (T-116v). Exporter keys ah0/ah2/arms. Layer 12-15 truth-half reference: 2.035e-8 (8M; -18%).
- T-116v LATE-LAYER NOISE LADDER (r35, 1e9; loop alone 2.407e-8): true v half at 12-15 -> 2.040e-8 (-15%); + iid 0.1% -> 2.572e-8 (+7%, the gain erased); + iid 0.3% -> 6.625e-8 (2.75x worse). A per-unit variance supply needs residual <~0.05% (~99% removal, below the 0.14% label noise): the PER-UNIT variance head is dead by physics. Running T-116y: iid diagonal noise vs STRUCTURED mean-aligned rank-2 covariance noise at matched induced size (struct_noise.sh) - if the loop tolerates structure, the covariance-level head (T-116x) is the form to pursue.
- T-116p/w RESULTS: HV2 (mean head retrained with the v-head frozen on rows with the head active): on those rows the one-step host is 1.61e-8 (the head at mix 1.0 cuts the oracle one-step error 28% teacher-forced) but the remainder is less predictable (48%) -> 8.34e-9; loops mix 1.0 6.17e-8 / 7.16e-8, mix 0.5 r35 4.63e-8: co-adaptation does not rescue the per-unit head. HVLOOP (head trained on loop rows only): held-out loop-state removal 70/78/80% (L12/14/15) but the loop at mix 0.5 r35 3.31e-8: still harmful. VERDICT: the per-unit variance head is dead in every form (v1, wider, loop-consistent, co-adapted, windowed) - consistent with the noise law (T-116v). Running T-116y (structured rank-2 noise vs diagonal noise) and T-116z (correlation-preserving scaling C <- D^1/2 C D^1/2 with the truth-half + 0.3% noise): which APPLICATION FORM tolerates imperfect supplies.
- T-116y/z (r35, 8M frame unless noted; loop alone 2.49e-8 8M / 2.407e-8 1e9): iid DIAGONAL v noise alone at 12-15: 0.1% -> 3.05e-8 (+22%), 0.3% -> 7.18e-8 (2.9x); STRUCTURED rank-2 (a m^T + m a^T, random a = 8e-5 ~ 0.1% induced) -> 2.67e-8 (+7%): ~3x more tolerant. Correlation-preserving true-v half at 12-15 (C <- D^1/2 C D^1/2) 2.055e-8 = same gain as the diagonal form; its 0.3%-noise run pending. HVLOOP loops 3.17e-8 / 4.66e-8 (1e9): dead. HV2 co-adapted (1e9): mix 0.5 4.52 / 4.86, mix 1.0 6.06 / 7.30: dead. ACOV (rank-2 covariance head, 40 ep) training -> loops at mix 0.5 full depth and 12-15.
- T-116y STRUCTURED NOISE (1e9, r35, loop alone 2.407e-8): rank-2 mean-aligned random a: induced ~0.1% -> 2.577e-8 (+7%); ~0.3% -> 4.071e-8 (+69%); diagonal iid: 0.1% -> 2.988e-8 (+24%), 0.3% -> 7.158e-8 (+197%). Structure buys ~3x tolerance but a 0.3%-class residual still hurts badly; a covariance-level head needs induced residual <~0.1% (~85-90% accuracy). The natural error's residual (true half leaves 0.35% at L14) is tolerated because it is the natural pattern - size is not the criterion, direction is.
- T-116z CORRELATION-PRESERVING application (C <- D^1/2 C D^1/2) + 0.3% noise -> 6.70e-8 = the diagonal form's 6.63e-8: the application form does not matter for iid noise; per-unit randomness is what the recursion amplifies. HVBIL loops (8M): mix 0.5 3.26e-8 / 4.65e-8, mix 1.0 6.26e-8: dead. PIVOT (T-117): feed the variance channel with DETERMINISTIC structured supplies that fix the SOURCE of the covariance error - (a) the frozen fourth-order plane scale table (T-71, K22/K31, beta & ratio; zero cost) in the honest loop (plane_in_loop.sh), (b) the engine's K22 plane chain in-card (RETRO_K22_CHAIN=1, GEMM-class; T-89 cut the F2J-off host to 3.3/3.8e-8 from 7.9e-8) in the loop (k22chain_in_loop.sh). If either helps, retrain the mean head on rows generated with it active (co-adaptation), then loop, then gate.
- T-117 PLANE SCALE (beta) IN THE HONEST LOOP r35: 9.96e-5 - DIVERGES (4000x). The frozen K22/K31 scale table is not usable inside the closed loop as is (numerical blow-up, not mere co-adaptation). Ratio mode / r100 and the engine K22 chain pending. HVBIL final (1e9): mix 0.5 3.10/4.61, mix 1.0 6.07/8.89: dead.
- T-117 RESULTS: plane scale (both modes, both nets) DIVERGES in the loop (1e-4 class); engine K22 chain in-card, un-adapted head, r35 6.13e-8 (8M; loop alone 2.49e-8) -> a deterministic source fix needs co-adaptation of the mean head to be judged. T-117e second-stage probe (cross-layer ridge on the loop's own rows, 816 features): 17% of the pre-final-correction residual, of which 15% from the final layer's own features -> no cross-layer information; dead (consistent with T-112). T-117f mean-head gap: train 67.9% vs valid 66.9% -> no model variance, ensembling useless; pure information limit. T-118 PIVOT: utilization (0.61 vs the competitor's 0.22) is the structural gap - at util 0.22 our raw 2.5e-8 would adjust to 5.5e-9. Test a cheaper card inside the honest loop (CP3 cap 8192 = half the default 16384; CP4 cap 1024/512 vs 2048), raw x util, then co-adapt the mean head to the cheaper card if the product improves (cheap_card_in_loop.sh). Also queued: co-adaptation to the K22 chain (rows with the chain active, generator variant).
- T-117d k4 RESCALE (deterministic per-layer table, full, un-adapted head) r35 7.25e-8 (1e9): 3x worse (true k4 half was +20%). Every state change breaks the co-adapted mean head; only co-adapted comparisons are meaningful from here. Running: T-117g K22-chain co-adaptation (rows_meanleak_mech15_k22 generating with the chain active -> GNN_HK22 -> loop with the chain), T-118 cheap-card runs (CP3 cap 8192, CP4 cap 1024/512; raw x util).
- T-117g K22-chain co-adaptation DEFERRED: rows with the numpy chain active take ~10+ min each (6/200 after 25 min = ~6 h for the set); low prior (the chain's plane fix was 84% in-span of the patch in the open-loop era) and an undeployable numpy chain -> killed to free the CPU for the cheap-card runs. Resume only if the utilization line fails: launch_mech15_k22.sh + chain_k22_coadapt.sh.
- T-118 CP3 CAP in the honest loop (r35, 8M frame, un-adapted head): cap 16384 (default) util 0.604 raw 2.49e-8; cap 8192 util 0.546 (-10%) raw 4.46e-8. D11 open-loop cap screen (CAP_SCREEN_*.json): util 0.554 -> 0.542 (8192) -> 0.482 (4096) -> 0.433 (2048) with raw +351% / x67 / x90: the third-order rank cap is NOT where the cost lives (the census shows per-layer cost rising linearly with depth, 7.7e10 at L5 -> 1.25e11 at L14; total 1.32e12 = 0.60). Running the per-source-line FLOP census (K_sitecensus.py) on the D11 card to locate the 0.6.
- T-116x COVARIANCE-LEVEL HEAD (rank-2 mean-aligned, single per-unit factor) DEAD: after 40 epochs the induced-dv removal is 9-16% (negative at L8), the joint training degraded the per-unit v head to 22-24% and the MEAN head to 50.7% (train loss 3.2): the a m^T + m a^T form with the post mean does not fit the variance error, and its loss destabilises the shared encoder. Chain killed before its loops.
- T-118 CP4 CAP in the honest loop (r35, un-adapted head; 1e9 raw, u_local): cap 2048 (default) 2.407e-8 @ 0.604 (raw x util 1.454e-8); cap 1024 2.499e-8 @ 0.567 (1.417, -2.6%); cap 512 2.594e-8 @ 0.547 (1.418, -2.5%). The fourth-order bank buys ~10% util per halving at ~4-8% raw un-adapted; with co-adaptation the raw may return to ~2.41 -> ~-9% adjusted. Bar in these units 0.94e-8 (-35%): CP4 alone is not enough; mapping the rest of the util curve (CP4 cap 256/128/0) and the site census to find the remaining cost.
- T-118b CP4 CURVE (r35, 1e9 raw @ util, un-adapted head): 2048 2.407@0.604 (x=1.454); 1024 2.499@0.567 (1.417); 512 2.594@0.547 (1.418); 256 2.639@0.537 (1.417); 128 2.692@0.532 (1.432); off 2.839@0.527 (1.496). The whole fourth-order bank costs 0.077 util (13%) and is worth 18% raw un-adapted; the util FLOOR without it is 0.527 -> the bulk of the cost (0.53) is elsewhere (census running). Co-adaptation chain picks cap 1024/256 (raw x util 1.417) -> rows -> retrain -> loop.
- T-118d CARD CENSUS (card_census.py, D11, r35, 1.319e12 = 0.600): _cp3_winograd_arena_depth4 44.9% (the third-order bank push, 7^4-block Winograd; the cap does not shrink it), _push_quartic 13.7%, _cp4_read_planes 13.0%, _predict 10.1% (covariance push), _push_cubic 9.1%, _cp4_push 4.7%, _g_four_all 3.0%. Third-order machinery 54%, fourth-order 32%, covariance/mean 10%. The arena is the Strassen-optimal dispatch (phase-1 laws) and compression of the bank was CLOSED WITH RECEIPTS (T-95..T-97b: billed low-rank costs more than it saves; youngest cohorts are full rank). So the util floor of this card family is ~0.5 (arena 0.27 + quartic/covariance/cubic) and the fourth-order bank is the only cheap 13%.

Needs co-adaptation (rows with the chain active; ~10-15 min per row). Running a 60-row version (ranks 35-54, 101-120, 168-187) -> fine-tune from HV_F1 -> loop with the chain on r35/r100 -> 1e9 ranking (chain_k22_small.sh).

Unless it REPLACES the card's CP4 bank (32% of the budget), the chain would push util to ~0.8, where the bar needs raw < 1.2e-8 (1e9-frame ~1.1e-8). Judge the co-adapted raw first (chain_k22_small), price second.

The adjusted score of this family is bounded near -6% below the deployed card; the bar (adjusted < 1e-8, -44%) is out of reach for the moment-closure card + learned closure paradigm. Best deployable: LEAN-2 co-adapted (CP4 off + no quartic; util 0.45-0.46; 1e9 raw x util 1.36 / 1.56 vs 1.45 / 1.60).
- T-118i LEAN-2 CANDIDATE DOES NOT REPRODUCE THE INSTRUMENT: cand_gnn_lean2.py grader r35 1.33e-7 / r100 2.06e-7 (instrument loop 3.15e-8 / 3.51e-8 8M). OPEN; parked (the line is a small bank at best). All cheap-card numbers above are INSTRUMENT numbers.
- T-119 THE THIRD-ORDER PLANE IS LOW-RANK (r35, from the bank dumps bankcp_r35_L*.npz, K21 = P diag(lam) Q^T): energy at rank 64 / 128 / 256: L4 82.7 / 95.9 / 99.7%; L8 95.0 / 99.4 / 100%; L12 98.2 / 99.9 / 100%; L14 98.8 / 99.9 / 100%, while the bank carrying it has rank 4096-14336 and the arena push costs 45% of the budget. A rank-128 PLANE carry (push (W^T U) S (W^T V)^T, add the births, re-truncate by a randomized SVD) would cost ~1% of the budget. T-95..T-97 closed the COHORT-bank compression (per-cohort factor ranks, dense reads); this is different: the plane itself. Necessary-condition test: RETRO_PLANE_RANK=r truncates the plane at every layer (numpy emulation, unbilled) -> honest loop raw at r=128/64.
- T-119 first numbers (r35, un-adapted HF1 head, 8M): plane truncated at every layer to rank 128 -> 1.40e-7; rank 64 -> 3.08e-7 (loop alone 2.49e-8). As with every state change, meaningless before co-adaptation. COST MODEL of a real rank-r plane carry: push (W^T U) S (W^T V)^T = 2 x 1024^2 x r; births = the new cohort G (dense 1024x1024) added then re-truncated by a randomized SVD of a 1024 x (r+1024) matrix (~3e8 for r=128); read k3 = diag(U S V^T) trivial -> ~0.2-1% of the budget replacing the arena's 45% (the cubic star push 9% stays; with the fourth-order machinery also replaced by the closure, the card would sit near util 0.15-0.2, where the bar needs raw < ~5e-8 grader). Running chain_planerank.sh: plain host at rank 256/128, then co-adaptation at rank 128 (rows_meanleak_mech15_pr128 -> GNN_HPR128 -> loops).
- T-119b BUILT: a faithful (unbilled numpy) emulation of a rank-r PLANE CARRY (RETRO_PLANE_CARRY=r): after each reset the post plane k21_target is truncated to rank r and CARRIED; at the next layer pre_k21 = W^T K21_post W replaces the bank read + cubic star, so truncation errors compound as in a real card; plane_carry_err_l dumps the relative deviation from the bank's plane. Co-adaptation chain queued behind the read-truncation chain.
- T-119 CLOSED (my error): a faithful carry emulation (pre_k21 = W^T K21_post W from the truncated post plane) deviates 400-600% from the bank's plane at every layer and the host explodes (2e-2): the third-order object is a TENSOR carried as CP factors (K21_ab = sum_r lam_r p_ra^2 q_rb); its push acts on the factors (p -> W^T p, q -> W^T q), not as a congruence of the (a,a,b) plane. The plane's low matrix rank is a consequence of the factors (p^2 weighting), not a cheaper carrier; the cost is set by the factor bank's rank (the youngest cohorts' Q are full rank, T-95b) -> exactly the T-95..T-97 verdict: compression closed. Both plane chains killed. The read-truncation test is moot (no cheap representation produces the truncated plane).

Bar at util 0.233 needs raw < ~4.3e-8 grader -> the closure would have to remove ~98% of the host error in the loop (the stack card got 88% teacher-forced, 3e-7 loop). The missing terms are a known bilinear structure (the K21 plane's effect) - a definite test of whether the closure can learn the bank's function from the weight-graph messages. Co-adaptation round launched (chain_diagcard.sh: rows -> GNN_HDIAG (init HNQ) -> loops with util -> gate).

- T-121 PURE GAUSSIAN CARD (no third- or fourth-order machinery at all: RETRO_K21_ZERO + no cubic + CP4 off + no quartic; CP3 cap 1024 because cap 1 breaks the arena kernel): util 0.178, plain host 2.83e-6 (33x the full card; the diagonal card was 2.54e-6). - T-121 PURE GAUSSIAN CARD, co-adapted: oracle one-step host 3.11e-4 (the plain host was 2.8e-6: the oracle-recursion one-step error is far larger because the Gaussian recursion's covariance drifts without the third-order terms), closure 90.7% teacher-forced, loop r35 1.234e-6 @ 0.163 / r100 1.431e-6 @ 0.181 (1e9): stable but 50x the full card -> raw x util ~2.2e-7, 15x worse than the bank. - T-122 DESIGN NOTE (no compute; for Matt's decision): what a cheap third-order carry must satisfy, from the receipts. (1) The card USES only planes (K21, and the k3 diagonal) and the fourth-order planes; the tensor is carried only to produce the next layer's planes. (2) The plane push needs the tensor's factors (K21_ab of a pushed CP term = (W^T p)_a^2 (W^T q)_b); the youngest cohorts' transport factors are full rank (T-95b), so any factor-form carry costs O(rank x 1024^2) per layer - the arena is already the cheapest dispatch (T-97). (3) The competitor's util 0.22 ~ our Gaussian card (0.16-0.18) + ~0.04, i.e. his third-order content costs ~1e11 flops total ~ one or two 1024^3 matmuls per layer: he does NOT carry a factor bank. Candidates for a 1-2 matmul/layer third-order closure: (a) a Gaussian recursion with an analytic per-layer third-order correction of the MEAN only (k3 diagonal via the cubic star push, 9% - already tested as 'diag k3': the covariance recursion still needs the planes, host 2.5e-6, dead); (b) a correction of the COVARIANCE recursion that uses a learned or analytic low-rank plane surrogate (the K21 plane is rank 64-128) built from the Gaussian state and the weights at each layer (not carried): K21_l ~ f(W_l, m, C) rank-r; the question is whether a per-layer surrogate (no memory across layers) can reproduce the accumulated plane - T-115i's ladder says 2/3 of the final variance error is born inside the last pair map, i.e. the plane's effect is dominated by RECENT layers -> a short-memory surrogate (last 2-3 layers' births, exact, rank 1024 each, pushed 1-3 times = 2-6 matmuls/layer) may capture most of the third-order content. This is the first falsifiable test of a new family: truncate the CP bank by AGE (keep only the youngest k cohorts; RETRO_LOWRANK_CP3 can emulate an age cutoff by setting old ranks to 0) and measure the honest host and the co-adapted loop vs k. Cost of a k-cohort bank = k x 1024 rank -> arena ~ k/15 of 45% -> k=3: ~9% util; card ~0.3 with the fourth-order machinery replaced by the closure. If the co-adapted loop at k=3 holds within ~1.5x of the full card's raw, adjusted ~1e-8. One round (~1 h) - awaiting Matt's go.
- T-122 AGE-TRUNCATED BANK PROBE (plain card, r35, emulated via RETRO_LOWRANK_CP3 with rank 0 for old cohorts): youngest 3 cohorts -> F2J-off host 6.42e-7 (7.5x the full 8.62e-8), youngest 5 -> 3.09e-7 (3.6x). The third-order plane content is NOT recent-dominated (the ladder's "born in the last pair map" was the variance ERROR's birth, not the plane's content); with the closure at ~90% the loop would still be 2-4x the full card's raw for ~30-40% less cost -> worse adjusted. Design fails its necessary condition; no round. No compute running.
- T-123 NEW-FAMILY TEST 1 (GPU only, no card runs): the competitor's util 0.22 ~ the Gaussian card (0.16-0.18) + ~0.04 = the cost of a ~1M-parameter per-unit corrector per layer, not a tensor bank. Our 64-channel closure reaches 90.7% teacher-forced on the Gaussian card's rows (host 3.11e-4); the bar at util 0.18 needs ~98% in the loop. Does capacity move it? Train 256-channel / 48-message closures on rows_meanleak_mech15_gauss (existing) and read the held-out removal curve (train_gnn_hgauss_c256.log).
- T-123 CAVEAT: the Gaussian card's ORACLE rows are unstable at the last layers (one-step error L1-L10 1.6-5e-7 = the true Gaussian fresh error, then L12 1.5e-6, L14 9.4e-6, L15 2.4e-4: the consistent covariance update with the Gaussian card's large mean corrections blows the covariance up). The 90.7% "final removal" and the loop's 1.2e-6 are on/against corrupted late-layer targets; judge the capacity test by the INTERIOR layers (L8/L12: 46% / 22% at 64ch). A future Gaussian-card row set needs a damped consistent update (RETRO_MEANLEAK_MIX < 1 or no delta-delta term) - only if the family continues.
- T-123 FIRST SIGNAL: on the Gaussian card's rows, a closure with 48 MESSAGE channels (x3 edge powers) and 256 hidden (128 state channels, from scratch) removes L8 54% / L12 56% / L14 66% at epoch 5, vs the 64-channel baseline 46 / 22 / 8 and the 256-state-channel run 45 / 20 / 12 (epoch 5). The width of the messages through W, W^2, W^3 - the capacity to carry the third-order HISTORY - is the binding dimension, not the per-unit state. Next: message width 128 / two message hops per layer; stable Gaussian-card rows (damped consistent update) for honest final-layer targets; then the loop at util 0.18 (bar: raw < ~5.6e-8 grader).
- T-123b: the Gaussian card's oracle-recursion instability is the CONSISTENT COVARIANCE UPDATE going indefinite (min eigenvalue -1.5e-3 at L2, -0.16 at L11, -2.2 at L14: the rank-2 mixed term -(m d^T + d m^T) has eigenvalues +-|m||d| and the card's covariance has no margin). PSD projection after the update (RETRO_MEANLEAK_PSD=1) fixes it: r35 final one-step 3.4e-4 -> 6.2e-7 (= the interior level 4-7e-7). Generating rows_meanleak_mech15_gausspsd (200 nets, 6 workers) for honest late-layer targets. Message-width curve at epoch 20: 48 msg ch -> L8 57 / L12 68 / L14 77%; 256 state ch -> 53 / 25 / 18%; 64 ch baseline 46 / 22 / 8%.
- T-123c: fnp parity of the in-loop step at the new widths (48 msg ch, 256 hidden, 128 state): max |fnp - torch|/rms 8e-7..2e-6 per layer (float32 level); the closure's own bill 1.56e10 flops = 0.71% of the budget (0.21% at 64 ch). Parity gotcha: gnn_parity.py's torch stage builds the model from the env, so GNN_FIRST/GNN_CM/GNN_HIDDEN must be set (without GNN_FIRST=1 the torch model starts at layer 2 and the "mismatch" is 27x rms at L2 - not a real failure). Message width 48 vs 128 at epoch 45: 59/71/83 vs 60/73/83 (L8/12/14): saturated at 48. The state-width run (256 ch, 16 msg) was stopped at epoch 35: 55/26/24.
- T-123 VERDICT (new family test, honest): on STABILISED Gaussian-card rows (PSD-projected consistent update; one-step final host 5.9e-7, smooth profile), the 48-message closure removes 60-64% at EVERY layer (peak 64.3% at epoch 20, overfits to 58% by 60; L8/12/14 = 62/62/64) - the same ~62-65% information ceiling as the full card's closure. The 83%-at-L14 "message-width signal" was an artifact of the exploded late-layer targets (the explosion is a predictable function of the state). Closed loop with this closure on the Gaussian card: r35 1.32e-5 / r100 1.56e-5 at util 0.160 (DIVERGES vs the card's open loop 2.8e-6 and the 64-ch loop 1.2e-6: state mismatch between the projected rows and the unprojected loop). Bar needed ~98% removal. FAMILY CLOSED: a per-unit learned closure has a ~62-65% ceiling on any moment card, cheap or rich. The "competitor = Gaussian card + learned corrector" hypothesis is falsified for this corrector class.
- T-123 ROW-HEALTH AUDIT (one-step error profile L10 -> L15 over 12 nets): HEALTHY (flat): mech15b (full card), nq (no-quartic), cap (CP4 256), lean2, gausspsd. EXPLODED (indefinite consistent covariance update): lean (97x), diag (562x), cp3low (65x), gauss (unprojected, 500x). Teacher-forced removal numbers on the exploded sets (HDIAG, HCP3LOW, Hlean, HGAUSS 90.7%) are VOID; the no-quartic 87% and LEAN-2 numbers stand. Standing habit: every new oracle row set gets this profile check before any training is read.
- T-124 DESIGN (the one route named and never built: the DIFFERENTIABLE CARD, Matt's preauth memo + the Keenan mechanism): a torch port of the GAUSSIAN card (mean + full covariance recursion, the card's own 37-term Hermite/Mehler pair map, W C W^T pushes) on MPS, and a rank-k covariance-plus-mean compensator per layer TRAINED THROUGH THE LOOP against the 1e9 truth post-means (150 train / 50 valid nets). Why it is not the closed family: the closed closure is per-unit and teacher-forced (62-65% ceiling; state mismatch kills co-adaptation in the loop); the loop-trained compensator optimises the final error directly and corrects the COVARIANCE (structured supplies are the only ones the laws allow). Milestones: M1 the torch card reproduces the fnp Gaussian card's open-loop prediction on r35 (2.8e-6 class) to ~1e-3 relative; M2 the compensator's held-out final error vs the bar 4.5e-8 (1e9 frame) at util ~0.17-0.19; the teacher-forced closure's loop is 1.2e-6. GPU only; no card workers.
- T-124 M1 PASSED: diff_card.py (torch, float64 CPU, 0.9 s per net) reproduces the fnp Gaussian card's open-loop per-layer post means to 4e-7 relative and variances to 3e-6 (r35; final prediction rel 2.3e-4; final mse vs truth 2.897e-6 vs the instrument's 2.880e-6). Conventions: weights (in,out) row-vector (pre = post @ W), x ~ N(0,I), Mehler 37 terms off-diagonal + exact raw2 diagonal. The card is now differentiable end to end.
- T-124 M2 first reads (through-the-loop, held-out 50 nets, plain Gaussian card 3.78e-6): DC1 (per-unit heads, 64 hidden, one W hop) 67.4% after ONE epoch, 68.5% at epoch 5 (1.19e-6) then flat; DC2 (256 hidden, 16 msg x W/W^2/W^3, lr 2e-3) 67.9% at epoch 1. The same ~65-68% as every teacher-forced closure: the ceiling is a property of PER-UNIT corrections, not of the training regime. Reading: the Gaussian card's mean error is the missing third-order content; the diagonal part (T_aaa, via the W^3 message) is per-unit-expressible, the OFF-DIAGONAL part (the K21 plane / the CP factor bank, 45% of the full card's cost) is not - a per-unit head with linear messages cannot form sum_ab W_ai^2 W_bi T_aab. DC3: a RECURRENT per-unit state (64 channels) transported through W, W^2, W^3 with energies = a LEARNED factor bank (a CP factor p transports as W^T p), heads from the state, trained through the loop. Deploy cost ~0.3% util.
- T-124 M2 CLOSED: four compensator designs trained THROUGH the differentiable Gaussian card (per-unit small/large, W-power messages, recurrent 64-ch state, rank-32 covariance) all plateau at 66-68.6% held-out removal (1.19-1.28e-6 vs plain 3.78e-6). Per-unit corrections of a moment card cap at ~2/3 regardless of training regime (teacher-forced or through the loop). Stopped.
- T-125 (plane_ansatz_test.py, MPS MC 1M samples, net 35, zero billed): the K21 PLANE (two-equal-index third cumulants P_ab = k3(h_a,h_a,h_b), 1024^2/layer) transported by the CORRECT ansatz  k3(z_i) = sum_a W_ai^3 T_aaa + 3 sum_{a!=b} W_ai^2 W_bi P_ab  (= 3 diag((W∘2)^T P W) + (W∘3)^T T; three GEMMs per layer) reproduces the next layer's per-unit pre-activation SKEW at corr 0.948/0.966/0.961/0.964 and scale 0.91-0.95 (L3->4, 7->8, 11->12, 14->15); residual rel 0.26-0.32 (label noise 0.03-0.12). Diagonal-only: corr 0.38-0.57 (useless, = T-120). CORRECTION TO T-119: the "plane carry deviates 400-600%" emulation (T-119b) transported the plane by CONGRUENCE (pre = W^T K21 W), which is the wrong physics (drops the W∘2 term); the plane line was closed on a wrong formula. The all-distinct part of the tensor is ~7% of the skew variance. This reopens the cheap third-order carry: plane transport 3 GEMMs/layer (~0.3% util each) instead of the 45% CP bank.
- T-125b: exact index-pattern transport of the (non-symmetric) plane: corr 0.960/0.956 scale 0.87 (L7->8, L14->15) vs the symmetric-P formula 0.965/0.964 scale 0.92; both leave a rel residual 0.26-0.30 = the all-distinct part, which UNDER-predicts the skew coherently by 8-13% (a learned scale could absorb part). Expected plane-card raw before any closure ~ (0.27)^2 x 2.9e-6 ~ 2e-7 (third-order term only). Instrument hook RETRO_PLANE_ANSATZ=1 (with RETRO_PLANE_CARRY=1024) implements the transport; run on r35 with the full card otherwise.
- T-125c RESULT (r35, full card with the third-order bank REPLACED by the plane-ansatz carry, transport unbilled): raw 8.43e-7 F2J-off at util 0.6005 (the bank still billed in the emulation). Carried pre-plane vs the bank-built pre-plane: rel deviation 0.46-0.50 at EVERY layer (vs 4-6x for the congruence). So the plane ansatz alone is 3.4x better than the Gaussian card (2.9e-6) and 30x worse than the bank (2.9e-8): the all-distinct third-order content (27% of the skew rms, 50% of the plane) is worth 30x in raw. A pure plane card at the util floor would score ~8e-8 adjusted (worse than the bank). Next: the HYBRID - a rank-capped bank (CP3_CAP=1024, real util) + the plane-ansatz transport of the PLANE RESIDUAL the truncated bank loses (post plane minus the bank's own post read).
- T-125d HYBRID round 1 (r35, CP3_CAP=1024 + plane-residual ansatz): DIVERGED (mse 8e14). The residual I defined (post plane minus the bank's own post read of the OLD factors) grows to 100% by L7 because the bank is REBORN from the post plane each layer (births): the residual is the births' plane, which the bank then carries too -> double counting -> positive feedback. Also measured: util at CP3_CAP=1024 is 0.437 (full 0.600): the bank-dependent cost is ~0.16, the rest of the card's third-order cost is fixed (reads/planes). A hybrid needs the residual AFTER births (= the truncation loss only) - requires the bank lifecycle (births/transport/read). Cost-correct clean design: Gaussian recursion 0.03 + plane ansatz ~0.05 + a rank-256 bank for the all-distinct remainder ~0.01 = util ~0.1 -> needs raw < 1e-7; the plane alone gives 8.4e-7, the full bank 2.9e-8; whether a rank-256 bank of the all-distinct REMAINDER is enough is the open question (never measured; the cap-2048 result capped the WHOLE tensor).

- T-128 THE ORGANIZERS' METHOD IS PUBLIC (found 2026-09-06 via the phase-2 write-up's references): Wu, Lecomte, Winer, Robinson, Hilton, Christiano, "Estimating the expected output of wide random MLPs more efficiently than sampling", arXiv:2605.05179; code github.com/alignment-research-center/mlp_cumulant_propagation (package mlp_kprop: kprop_harmonic.py, factor_k3.py, factor_k4.py, diagslice.py, flop_utils.py). Cumulant propagation ("kprop") to order K with POWER CUMULANTS (Hermite expansions of powers of the activation: the coincident-index correction), an extra full trace for odd K, and a FACTORIZED representation of the K-th cumulant of size n^(K-1) preserved through each step (runtime c L^2 n^K for K>=3): MSE ~ c_K (L/n)^K. At n=256, 4 hidden layers: K=3/4 factorized beat MC by 10-100x; at 8 hidden layers K=4 starts to lose (depth hurts). The seven phase-2 teams at util ~0.2 are almost certainly kprop K=3 (factorized, maybe augmented) ported to flopscope; their raw ~2e-8 at 1024x16. Our CP bank carries every cohort exactly (13 cohorts); kprop MERGES at each nonlinearity via the leading 1/n diagrams -> one n^2 object per order. NEXT: run their torch code on our nets (K=3 factorized / augmented) -> raw vs the 1e9 truth + their FLOP count; then port/beat.
- T-128b: the organizers' kprop run on OUR net 35 (run_kprop_ours.py; their torch code, MPS float32, their FLOP counter with symmetric-tensor discounts): K=2 (Gaussian) final mse 3.02e-6 at 0.033 of 2^41 (= our Gaussian card 2.9e-6; confirms the Gaussian recursion costs ~3%); K=3 factorized SIMPLE: 3.20e-7 at 1.586 of 2^41 (10x better than Gaussian, 10x WORSE than our card's 2.9e-8, and 60% over budget). Vanilla kprop K=3 is NOT the leaders' method (they are 17x more accurate at 1/8 the cost). Our card is a strong implementation of the same physics class. Checking float64 and AUGMENT.
- T-128c PRECISION: the organizers' kprop K=3 factorized in FLOAT64 (CPU, 8 s!): final mse 3.447e-8 on r35 (float32 on MPS gave 3.20e-7: a 10x precision loss). So vanilla kprop K=3 = our card's accuracy class (ours 2.9e-8 board / 2.4e-8 1e9-frame r35) at 1.59 budgets by their symmetric-discounted counter (ours 0.6 by flopscope). K=2 AUGMENT: 2.90e-6 at 0.038 (no gain). The leaders (1.85e-8 at 0.218) are ~8x cheaper than vanilla K=3 and 1.9x better: consistent with the organizers' hint = a RANK-REDUCED K=3 (factors in the top covariance directions) + something for the accuracy. NEXT: kprop's per-op cost breakdown; the rank parameter of its factored K3 representation; MSE vs rank at zero cost.

- T-130 PACKAGING BUG FOUND: every lean payload (payload_gnn_hlean2 / hcap256 / hnq, HV lineage) carries a VARIANCE HEAD (msgv_w, vrms). Proof chain: null closure identical to 1e-16; with closure: post mean/var/k3/k4/gate/covariance after L1 identical (covariance to 2.6e-8), L2 pre_mean and pre_k3 identical, L2 pre_variance differs 4.7e-4 = the v-head's diagonal write. - T-134 DESIGN FROM THE PUBLIC RECORD (Team Puffi, rank 4 in phase 2; phase-1 algorithmic PDF "Separate-Universe Response Closure", sub 327725): keep a K=3-class carrier (mean, covariance, CP third-cumulant columns) but REPLACE the fourth-order machinery by a covariance-RESPONSE closure: k4_ijkl = sum_ab Lambda_ab Sym(B_a (x) B_b) from p<=4 live modes B_1 = C, B_2 = Q/vbar (Q = P_off[c0 vbar C + c1 C∘C + c2/2 (D_m K^T + K D_m) + c3 (t m^T + m t^T) + c4 v v^T + c5 vbar^2 11^T], K = K21 plane, t = k3 diag), B_3 = vbar I, B_4 = 1/2 (g_i+g_j) C_ij; per-unit next pre-k4 = 3 q_i^T Lambda q_i with q_ai = w_i^T B_a w_i; Lambda (4x4, indefinite allowed) and the six c's fitted offline per layer by ridge to the measured next-layer marginal k4 (they: 30-net panel; we have vlabels mc_pre_k4 for 200 nets), frozen; plus a 31->32->32->1 late-layer drift corrector and a one-step response memory (beta=-0.3). Phase-1 result: layer-32 MSE 77% below factorized K=3 and 48% below FULL factorized K=4 at ~0.5B (K=4 costs 10^2 B). For OUR card: the fourth-order machinery is 21% of util (quartic push 13.7 + CP4 reads 13 + CP4 push 4.7 of 0.6); an SSC supply of pre_k4 / K31 / K22 costs ~6 GEMMs/layer (~10% util) or less. If raw holds: util 0.6 -> ~0.45; if Puffi's -48% vs K=4 transfers: raw 2.9 -> ~1.5e-8 => adjusted ~7e-9 = COMPETITIVE. First read (zero cost): can the 4-mode form represent the true next-layer k4(z_i) at width 1024 (R^2 per layer, true states, torch)? Then fit on CARD states, emulate in the instrument (host, F2J-off) at CP4_CAP=1 + NO_QUARTIC vs base 7.85e-8, then co-adapt the closure, package.
- T-134a SSC REPRESENTATION TEST (ssc_k4_test.py, r35, 131k MC samples, true post states): the 4-mode covariance-response form (9 features incl. the six Q terms, 45 products, ridge, held-out units) explains the next layer's per-unit k4 with R^2 only 0.02 / 0.26 / 0.43 / 0.53 at L4/8/12/14 (3 canonical modes 0.06-0.44; diagonal transport negative). At width 1024 the fourth cumulant is NOT a low-mode covariance response; Puffi's phase-1 gain (depth 32, width 256) does not transfer as a representation. An SSC-style replacement of our fourth-order machinery (worth 4x at the host) would recover at most half. Design T-134 downgraded (not dead: fitting to the MEAN effect rather than k4 was their better variant, +13.6%).
